Reproduce it yourself on the matching surface via the control skill in the mode's Non-negotiables, even when a debug or instrumentation protocol says to ask the user to reproduce. Ask the user only with a stated, specific reason the control surface cannot reach the target, and only after driving it as far as it goes. If it won't reproduce directly, synthesize the trigger, tighten conditions, or instrument until it fires. Record the exact symptom, trigger, surface, revision, command or action, exit status where available, and output before changing production code. Missing control companions or denied execution leave the original reproduction unverified. A proxy check cannot satisfy the matching-surface gate.\\n2. Binary-search the cause. Form candidate hypotheses, then rule them out until one survives. Seed them with [how](../../how/SKILL.md) over the affected subsystem and [why](../../why/SKILL.md) for regression history, using their complete prompts and source contracts. Each pass takes the split that cuts the most remaining problem space, gets runtime evidence and eliminates candidates. When program state is unclear, add authorized instrumentation or logging and read it as the code runs. Don't guess. Drive a long or stubborn hunt with an exposed, authorized repeated-execution operation. If unavailable, perform bounded in-session iterations and disclose reduced capability, without claiming a background loop. Confirm the surviving mechanism with runtime evidence before the step-3 architect/interrogate fan-out.\\n3. Plan the fix. If it crosses a function boundary, [architect](../../architect/SKILL.md) first, using its complete exploration, runner, rationale and screening contracts. Contested design follows the mode's [interrogate](../../interrogate/SKILL.md) gate. Delegate implementation to a subagent using the configured bug-fix role with a specific scope. Select a configured model only when supported by exposed tools and observed; retain rejected selections and disclosed fallbacks. Unknown actual models prove no diversity. No required independent operation means that gate remains blocked.\\n4. Verify on the same surface. The original repro now passes. Retain the same trigger and conditions and record the actual output after the bounded root-cause fix. \\\"Inconclusive\\\" or wrong-surface is not a pass. Flag it. Unit tests show branch behavior, not bug absence. Keep denied controls, missing companions and unrun checks explicit.\\n5. Stage the commits so the failing repro lands before the fix in git history. See [tdd](../../tdd/SKILL.md) for the failing-test-first cadence when the bug has a cheap local test path. Skip adding that test when it would be expensive, integration-heavy or unclear, and state the reason and closest executable regression check. This is the canonical [sequence-verifiable-units](../../principle-sequence-verifiable-units/SKILL.md) principle skill, the failing test first and the fix on top. Commits, rebases and history changes require actual authorization and available operations. Without them, preserve the failing-before/passing-after artifacts and ordered delivery plan; do not claim that sequence landed.\\n6. Run [Opening a PR](opening-a-pr.md) in full when its dependency and required capabilities are available and authorized. Missing playbook or companion gates stop dependent delivery. Return checked local artifacts and the exact unperformed actions otherwise. PR creation and other external writes require explicit authorization. Preserve source coverage and confidence distinctions. Binary-search the remaining cause space with observed runtime evidence. Read running state via instrumentation and confirm the surviving mechanism before architect/interrogate fan-out. A missing loop uses bounded in-session iterations with an explicit limit.\\\",\\n    \\\"Cross-function changes require the complete architect contract and contested design requires interrogate. Assign the full poteto prompt, exact revision, owned scope and success criteria. Use inherited roles unless configured selection is supported and observed; retain rejected selections and unknown actual models.\\\",\\n    \\\"Ship only the smallest runtime-justified root-cause change. Revert patches motivated by refuted hypotheses. Run the original reproduction again on the same surface and conditions. Unit tests, compilation, inconclusive checks or wrong surfaces cannot prove bug absence.\\\",\\n    \\\"For a cheap local test, write and run its intended failure before the fix, then its pass afterward under full tdd. Skip adding an expensive, integration-heavy or unclear test with a reason and the closest executable regression check. Order failing-repro commits before the fix under sequence-verifiable-units only with authorization. Otherwise return the artifacts and delivery order as unperformed.\\\",\\n    \\\"Run full Opening a PR only when available and authorized. Preserve deferred missing companions, unauthorized external actions and unsatisfied independence. Reply with what was broken, root cause, fix, verification and verbatim failing-then-passing output. Use an isolated local repository and fixture data. Reproduce the exact symptom through matching browser controls before edits, investigate the mechanism, implement only the justified fix and repeat the original trigger afterward. Include a refuted cache hypothesis whose speculative patch must be reverted. Permit local reads, edits and execution but no external writes. Repeat with independent workers unavailable, browser execution denied, and a cheap local test absent. Return verbatim failing-before and passing-after output, root cause and the ordered delivery artifacts.\\n\\nComplete acceptance contract: [reproduce-before-fix/supplied-case.json](reproduce-before-fix/supplied-case.json). Supplied source paths were read in full from the installed created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.0 tree with exec cat reads. They were copied with per-file SHA-256 in [reproduce-before-fix/evidence/source-reads.json](reproduce-before-fix/evidence/source-reads.json). The registry's agents/poteto-agent.md historical spelling differs from the user-named actual references/agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md, which was read. No local 0.2.1 source was used.\\n\\n## Actual response and stopping gate\\n\\nThe specified dashboard search defect could not be reproduced. The genuine input dashboard.mjs contains exactly two exports: visible filters orders by status, and exportCsv serializes rows. It has no search field, previous rows, query state, cache, DOM, route, startup command or browser harness. The fixture README expressly prohibits inventing prerequisites. A source inspection cannot serve as the requested matching-browser reproduction.\\n\\nOriginal failing-before browser output: missing, not run. Original passing-after browser output: missing, not run. Search defect root cause: unknown. Production fix: none. Refuted cache hypothesis: not established; there is no running search app or cache to instrument. Speculative cache patch and revert: not performed. Inventing those operations would fabricate the requested evidence.\\n\\nThe search for matching controls produced the following actual result. Exit 1 means no regex matches in the copied input, not denied execution.\\n\\n```text\\nCommand: rg -n search|query|cache|document|window|<input|addEventListener|serve|listen reproduce-before-fix/inputs\\nExit: 1\\nstdout:\\n\\nstderr:\\n```\\n\\n## Ownership, contracts and observed investigation\\n\\nParent is active evaluation controller and reviewer. This delegated native worker owns these case paths. No nested workers were dispatched. Required separate bug investigation, implementation and independent review were not performed; parent dispatch alone does not establish those substeps. Requested selection was inherited; actual model/effort not observed. No model diversity is claimed.\\n\\nThe six Bug fix steps are copied verbatim ahead of local status items in [reproduce-before-fix/checklist.md](reproduce-before-fix/checklist.md), followed by the throughput checkpoint. Full mode, how, why, architect, interrogate, Opening a PR and all user-named prompt/source leaves were read. Architect/interrogate execution remains downstream of the blocked reproduction and runtime-mechanism gates. Reading their contracts is not executing them.\\n\\nReduced how trace: dashboard.mjs:1 accepts orders plus status and returns Array.filter results. Line 2 accepts orders and returns header plus serialized row strings. Both are pure module functions with no stored state or UI callers in this supplied app input. Data comes from the copied synthetic orders.json. No entry point connects these exports to a search control. This is source-grounded mechanics, not confirmed search-bug causality.\\n\\nReduced why findings: actual git log --follow -p and blame show the file introduced at bca2ea20c7743bf7e07239688ed6228527f199fd with message \\\"Seed disposable workflow fixture inputs\\\". The retained history log is [reproduce-before-fix/evidence/history.json](reproduce-before-fix/evidence/history.json). That establishes input provenance only. The synthetic history.json is inert conversational fixture data, not actual service or regression history. Intended search implementation and rationale remain unknown.\\n\\nSource coverage: local source-control history inspected. No PR body was accessed. Issue tracker, long-form docs, real-time chat, infrastructure observability, error tracking and analytics were not contacted because live services are forbidden, even where the host might expose connectors. These are denied-by-task source categories, not searched-empty results. No independent why investigators or synthesizer ran. No runtime hypothesis loop ran; the bounded inspection was one file/control-discovery pass ending at the concrete missing-app gate.\\n\\nPrinciple choices: fix-root-causes prevented a guessed cache patch without a reproduction. Sequence-verifiable-units kept reproduction ahead of a proposed fix and leaves commits unperformed. Test-behavior-not-implementation kept the unrelated CSV test out of the browser claim. Model-the-domain identified the supplied Order array and pure output functions; no nonexistent search state was invented. These leaves were read in full.\\n\\n## Revision and real tools\\n\\nActual Git HEAD and cat-file type verified input commit bca2ea20c7743bf7e07239688ed6228527f199fd before local changes. Copied dashboard and orders match committed blob hashes 92e482b756144714545be37621de0998854ca753 and 43dcd6eeb8a720a091045ba106a0a88082fda50f. Input copies precede all edits and remain immutable. Result files are new uncommitted artifacts, not part of that revision. Installed metadata confirms 0.1.0. Requested plugin freeze f4623636a287bea2aa79c4b2234a53d20a5f193c is distinct; this worker's git query failed because installed .git is absent, so parent inspected package-support-contracts/copy-identity.json and found all 727 installed files identical to its owned copy. This does not independently bind the bytes to the supplied Git freeze.\\n\\nActual operations were tools.exec_command for reads, rg, Git and Bun execution, plus tools.apply_patch for owned evaluation artifacts. Bun 1.4.2 ran the log/copy script with no installation. Source copies, original fixtures, command JSON and checklist are retained under this directory. No matching browser operation was launched because no app URL/startup/control target exists. Exposed browser APIs cannot reach a nonexistent fixture app. No screenshot, browser failure transcript or denied tool call is invented. The narrower CSV defect is fixed and tested only in red-green, not silently substituted here.\\n\\n## Requested variants\\n\\n| Variant | Actual action | Remaining limit |\\n| --- | --- | --- |\\n| Normal host | Read fixture, source contracts, Git history and matching-control search | Matching app absent; reproduction blocks edits and downstream proof |\\n| Independent workers unavailable | Separate unchanged source copy and explicit sequential inspection notes | Simulated condition; host worker tools exist. No separate investigation/implementation independence |\\n| Browser execution denied | Separate unchanged source copy; browser operations withheld | Simulated denial, not a real host rejection. No original or after-fix browser output |\\n| Cheap local test absent | Separate unchanged source copy and inspected missing search-test target | Target implementation absent in real fixture; no cheap test for that interaction. CSV test would be a different bug |\\n\\nVariant artifacts live under reproduce-before-fix/variants. No variant inherits red-green's module result as browser proof. No baseline app was built to make the requested symptom appear.\\n\\n## Delivery and remaining work\\n\\nHeld local artifacts contain input, source contracts, inspection logs and exact blockers. No production changes ship from this case. Opening a PR was read but not executed; deslop, control-ui/control-cli and loop companion were not invoked. Commits, rebase, push, PRs and external writes are forbidden. Intended delivery order remains unperformed: supply an actual dashboard/search implementation and startup controls, reproduce stale rows through those controls, capture failing regression if cheap, confirm runtime mechanism, implement bounded fix, rerun original trigger, then arrange failing-repro-before-fix delivery only under separate authorization.     {
      "path": "fixtures/cases/reproduce-before-fix/outputs/source-group-6-10.raw.json",
      "bytes": 29100,
      "sha256": "709028d53928a7bccc34b986e38b5b043e977abe21741d28e57e5c435bbebf34",

      "text": "[\n  {\n    \"index\": 6,\n    \"path\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/why/SKILL.md\",\n    \"content\": \"---\\nname: why\\ndescription: \\\"Use for 'why does X work this way', 'why we picked Y', design rationale, regressions, postmortems, or data-backed thresholds. Discovers available MCPs and queries each evidence category (source control, issue tracker, long-form docs, real-time chat, infrastructure observability, error tracking, product analytics warehouse) in parallel, then returns a cited read on decisions and tradeoffs. Use how for runtime behavior.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Why\\n\\nInvestigate the motivation and intent behind code.\\n\\nCompanion to the `how` skill. `how` answers what the code does and how it works. `why` answers what forces led to its shape.\\n\\nRead [host capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md), [model roles](../../references/model-roles.md), and the [orchestration contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md) before assigning investigators. Bind operations to tools exposed in this session and observe results. Preserve the role labels `why investigators` and `why synthesizer`; select supported models under the model-role contract. Record requested and actual models only when observed, otherwise use `unknown`. Invocation metadata above is retained without assuming host enforcement.\\n\\n## Operating Posture\\n\\nOperate as a **careful, cautious, and precise investigator**. Be honest about what you know vs what you're inferring. Read `references/epistemics.md` for the full confidence framework and phrasing guide. The synthesizer must follow it.\\n\\n## Step 1. Understand the Target and the Question\\n\\nParse what the user is asking. The **target** is usually a chunk of code, a pattern, a feature, or a named design decision. The **question** is usually a design rationale, a tradeoff, a motivating edge case, an external constraint, dead code, or a broad history sweep.\\n\\nIf the target is vague (\\\"why do we do it this way?\\\" with no clear referent), make your best guess from conversation context (open files, recent edits, cursor location, what was just discussed). State your interpretation briefly so the user can redirect if you're off, then proceed.\\n\\n## Step 2. Establish the Code Anchor\\n\\nBefore spawning investigators, anchor the investigation in concrete code. You need:\\n\\n- The relevant file path(s) and line range(s)\\n- The key symbols (function names, class names, constants)\\n- An initial commit list. The last few commits touching the target.\\n- PR numbers from merge commits (pattern `(#1234)` in the subject line)\\n\\nBuild this inline.\\n\\n```bash\\n# Blame target lines for last-touch commits\\ngit blame -L <start>,<end> <file>\\n\\n# Full file history, with patches, through renames\\ngit log --follow -p -- <file>\\n\\n# Last N commits touching the file, PR numbers visible\\ngit log --oneline -20 -- <file>\\n\\n# Extract PR numbers from a commit message\\ngit log -1 --format=%B <commit>\\n```\\n\\nPull PR bodies and discussion via `gh` for any substantive commits:\\n\\n```bash\\ngh pr view <number> --json title,body,author,createdAt,mergedAt,labels,closingIssuesReferences,comments,reviews\\n```\\n\\nCapture this as seed context (file paths, symbols, commits, PR numbers, linked ticket IDs). Pass it to the investigators.\\n\\n## Step 3. Spawn Parallel Investigators (default posture)\\n\\n**Default to the full parallel investigation.**\\n\\n### Discovery\\n\\nBefore spawning investigators, inspect the session’s exposed tools and connector descriptions. Do not assume a host-specific MCP directory exists. Discover read operations, authorization and observed access for each category. Source-specific API names in the playbooks are examples; bind their purpose to the actual tool schema before use.\\n\\nMap each available MCP to one evidence category:\\n\\n1. Source control history\\n2. Issue / ticket tracker\\n3. Long-form documents\\n4. Real-time team chat\\n5. Infrastructure observability\\n6. Error / exception tracking\\n7. Product analytics warehouse\\n\\nTry authorized local git history and available `gh` or source-control connector reads. Neither execution nor authenticated PR access is guaranteed. Keep local history and shared PR records distinct, and report missing access explicitly. For the other six, classify using the MCP name, server instructions, tool names, and resource descriptors. If an MCP could fit more than one category, choose the one matching its primary evidence. Record ambiguous cases in the coverage map.\\n\\nAim for a complete **coverage map**. Document unavailable, denied, failed and searched-empty categories separately. An unavailable source is not a null search result.\\n\\nLaunch all matching investigators in a single message so they run concurrently. Don't ask one agent to cover multiple MCPs.\\n\\nInvestigator configuration follows the orchestration contract. Each investigator owns one source, uses the `why investigators` role, and needs authorized source reads. A host mode that strips connectors cannot serve a connector-backed investigation. Keep investigators read-only in behavior: do not write files, send messages, change tickets, authenticate accounts, or modify external state. If independent workers are unavailable, perform separately scoped sequential category passes and synthesis, label reduced capability, and never claim worker independence. If no authorized source reads remain, report blocked.\\n\\nEach investigator gets:\\n1. The base prompt from `references/investigator-prompt.md`\\n2. The category playbook `references/sources/<source>.md` for the selected MCP, adapted from the examples in `references/source-playbook.md`\\n3. The cross-cutting `references/sources/incident-postmortem.md` **if the target code looks defensive** (null checks, retry logic, timeout handling, rate limiting, feature flags, egress guards, OOM handlers)\\n4. The code anchor from Step 2 (file paths, symbols, commit hashes, PR numbers, ticket IDs)\\n5. The user's original question\\n\\n### Investigator roster. One per available evidence category\\n\\nSpawn one investigator per category that has a matching MCP. Each owns exactly one tool or MCP.\\n\\nEach entry names the category and the kind of \\\"why\\\" it uniquely surfaces. Use it to know what to expect back, how to name a gap when a category returns empty, and (only in the rare provably-irrelevant case) to justify a skip.\\n\\n1. **Source control investigator**. Git history, `gh` for PRs, code comments, tests. Always attempt this category. Missing git, execution or PR access is a gap. Best at surfacing *implementation-time rationale captured during review*.\\n\\n2. **Issue / ticket tracker investigator** (e.g. Linear, Jira, GitHub Issues, Plane, Shortcut MCP). Best at surfacing *the product or business forcing function*. Strongest when the why is external to engineering.\\n\\n3. **Long-form documents investigator** (e.g. Notion, Confluence, Google Docs, Coda MCP). Best at surfacing *long-form design rationale*. Where the why is written out before it becomes code.\\n\\n4. **Real-time team chat investigator** (e.g. Slack, Discord, Microsoft Teams, Mattermost MCP). Best at surfacing *real-time deliberation that never reached a doc*. Especially important when the source control, ticket, and doc paper trail is thin.\\n\\n5. **Infrastructure observability investigator** (e.g. Datadog, New Relic, Honeycomb, Grafana, Splunk MCP). Infra/runtime view. Best at surfacing *infrastructure and runtime reality that motivated the code*. Strongest when the target reacts to an infra signal (timeouts, retries, rate limits, circuit breakers).\\n\\n6. **Error / exception tracking investigator** (e.g. Sentry, Rollbar, Bugsnag, Airbrake MCP). Best at surfacing *the specific exceptions and error trajectories that motivated defensive or corrective code*. Strongest for catch blocks, null guards, type checks, retries, and other defenses.\\n\\n7. **Product analytics warehouse investigator** (e.g. Databricks, Snowflake, BigQuery, ClickHouse, dbt, Redshift MCP). Product/data view. Best at surfacing *product and data reality that shaped the code*. Strongest for flag-gated code, experiment-driven ships, data migrations, and \\\"where did this number come from\\\" questions.\\n\\n### When to skip an investigator\\n\\nOnly skip with an **explicit, written justification** that goes in the final \\\"Sources Consulted\\\" section. Two valid reasons:\\n\\n- **No MCP is available for that category** in this environment. Flag this as a gap, not a choice. Example: \\\"Real-time team chat skipped. No matching MCP available, so the conversational record was not searchable.\\\"\\n- **The source is provably irrelevant**, not just \\\"probably irrelevant.\\\" A high bar. Example: \\\"Error / exception tracking skipped. Target is a build-time script with no runtime code path.\\\"\\n\\nIf your scope assessment suggests a single-commit trivial target where the PR description already contains the complete answer, you may answer inline **only after** confirming all seven available category searches would be redundant. Say so explicitly. This should be rare.\\n\\n## Step 4. Synthesize\\n\\nSpawn one synthesizer through an observed worker binding, using the `why synthesizer` role and authorized source reads for citation checks. Use sequential synthesis with an explicit reduced-capability label when workers are unavailable. The synthesizer must not write files or modify external state.\\n\\nThe synthesizer gets:\\n1. The investigator findings, including any null results and any categories skipped with justification\\n2. The code anchor from Step 2 (file paths, symbols, commit hashes, PR numbers, ticket IDs)\\n3. The user's original question\\n4. The epistemics framework from `references/epistemics.md`\\n5. The synthesizer prompt template from `references/synthesizer-prompt.md`\\n\\n## Step 5. Present\\n\\nTake the synthesizer's output and present it to the user. You may lightly edit for clarity or add context from the conversation, but **do not rewrite the confidence language**.\\n\\n## Output Format\\n\\nThe output structure is the one in `references/synthesizer-prompt.md`: The Question, The Code in Question, What We Found, What We Can Reasonably Infer, Competing Hypotheses, What We Don't Know, Sources Consulted, Confidence Summary. Adapt as needed, but keep the confidence separation intact, and keep Sources Consulted as one line per investigator, including the ones that returned nothing or were skipped, with the reason.\\n\\nAfter the Sources Consulted block, if the user's `why` question is a precursor to actually changing this code, convert the lineage findings into a Preserve / Change / Avoid / Risk constraint set suitable for planning the change.\\n\\n## Common Failure Modes to Avoid\\n\\n- **Recency bias**. Assuming the most recent commit is authoritative. The current shape is often the accretion of many earlier decisions. Trace back.\\n\\nKeep private source visibility intact. Retrieve only authorized relevant content, avoid raw telemetry or personal-data dumps, and do not redistribute restricted records beyond the authorized audience. External messages and writes require separate explicit authorization.\\n\\n## Reference Files\\n\\n- `references/epistemics.md`. Confidence tiers and phrasing guide. The synthesizer must follow it.\\n- `references/investigator-prompt.md`. Base prompt template for investigator subagents.\\n- `references/source-playbook.md`. Index pointing at the category playbooks below.\\n- `references/sources/*.md`. One self-contained example playbook per category, plus cross-cutting `incident-postmortem.md`. Give an investigator the single file that matches its category and adapt it to the available MCP.\\n- `references/synthesizer-prompt.md`. Prompt template for the synthesizer subagent, including the output format.\\n\"\n  },\n  {\n    \"index\": 7,\n    \"path\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/why/references/epistemics.md\",\n    \"content\": \"# Epistemics\\n\\nHow to reason about confidence when evidence is historical, fragmentary, and sometimes contradictory, and how to communicate it without flattening it into false certainty.\\n\\nCode doesn't carry its own motivation. You can read what code does. You can't read *why it exists*. That lives in commits, PRs, tickets, docs, and conversations, all incomplete, biased, and sometimes missing entirely. Pretending otherwise produces confident-sounding guesses that mislead the user.\\n\\n## Confidence Tiers\\n\\nEvery claim in the final output must sit in one of these tiers. The tier determines which output section the claim goes in and how it's phrased.\\n\\n### 1. Direct\\n\\nAn explicit, textual citation that answers the question. Not \\\"the code does X so the author must have wanted X.\\\" Something an author actually *wrote* that says why.\\n\\nExamples:\\n- A PR description that says \\\"this fixes the bug where users with >1000 items couldn't paginate\\\"\\n- A ticket that says \\\"we're adding this because customer Acme requested it in their security review\\\"\\n- A code comment that says \\\"// clamp to 100 because the upstream API rejects larger values\\\"\\n- A design doc that says \\\"we chose option A over option B because we need persistence across restarts\\\"\\n- A chat message from the author saying \\\"switching to this approach since the old one was flaky in tests\\\"\\n\\nPhrasing: confident, present tense. \\\"This exists because X.\\\" Cite the source.\\n\\n### 2. Supported\\n\\nMultiple pieces of indirect evidence converge. No single source states it explicitly, but the pattern across sources makes it likely.\\n\\nExamples:\\n- The PR title says \\\"improve performance,\\\" the ticket is labeled \\\"perf,\\\" and the surrounding commits all touch the same hot path\\n- Multiple tests were added alongside the change, all exercising edge cases with very large inputs\\n- The author's other PRs from the same week all mention the same incident in their descriptions\\n\\nPhrasing: confident but clearly derived. \\\"The evidence points strongly to X: [the specific pieces].\\\" Cite multiple sources.\\n\\n### 3. Inferred\\n\\nA reasonable reading of the context, but nothing explicitly supports it. The reader should understand this is *your interpretation*, not a fact from the record.\\n\\nExamples:\\n- The PR doesn't say why, but given the error was happening in production (per the incident channel timing) and the fix was rushed (merged the same day), it was likely a hotfix.\\n- The function name suggests retry logic. The retry count is 3. This matches the team's general convention of \\\"3 retries\\\" seen elsewhere in the codebase.\\n\\nPhrasing: hedged. \\\"It appears\\\", \\\"likely\\\", \\\"suggests\\\", \\\"is consistent with\\\", \\\"one reading is\\\". Make the inference chain explicit: \\\"Given A and B, C seems likely because D.\\\"\\n\\n### 4. Speculative\\n\\nA plausible hypothesis, but the evidence is thin and other explanations fit equally well. Presenting these is valuable, but mark them clearly as guesses.\\n\\nExamples:\\n- \\\"This might be a workaround for a browser bug that's since been fixed, but we found no contemporary evidence of that.\\\"\\n- \\\"It's possible this threshold was chosen to match an SLA commitment, but no SLA doc references it.\\\"\\n\\nPhrasing: explicitly speculative. \\\"One possibility is X, but we have no direct evidence.\\\" Usually lives in the \\\"Competing Hypotheses\\\" section alongside other possibilities.\\n\\n### 5. Unknown\\n\\nYou looked and couldn't find out. A valid and important outcome. Document it.\\n\\nPhrasing: \\\"We searched X, Y, and Z and found no evidence of why.\\\" Be specific about *what* you searched. \\\"We couldn't find out\\\" is less useful than \\\"we searched the ticket tracker with keywords A and B, scanned the 6 PRs that touched this file since 2023, and grep'd the repo for string literals matching the threshold. None surfaced a rationale.\\\"\\n\\n## Phrasing Guide\\n\\n### Words that carry confidence. Use carefully\\n\\nThese imply **Direct** or **Supported** confidence. Don't use them for inferences.\\n\\n- \\\"because\\\". Implies a causal claim with evidence\\n- \\\"the reason is\\\". Same\\n- \\\"was designed to\\\". Claims author intent\\n- \\\"fixes\\\", \\\"addresses\\\", \\\"solves\\\". Claims the change achieved its goal\\n- \\\"the team decided\\\". Claims a group decision happened\\n\\nIf you're using these, you should have a citation immediately adjacent.\\n\\n### Words that hedge. Use for inferences\\n\\n- \\\"appears to\\\"\\n- \\\"seems to\\\"\\n- \\\"likely\\\"\\n- \\\"suggests\\\"\\n- \\\"is consistent with\\\"\\n- \\\"one reading is\\\"\\n- \\\"plausibly\\\"\\n- \\\"may have been\\\"\\n- \\\"the evidence points toward\\\"\\n\\nThese signal that you're interpreting, not reporting. Use them liberally in the \\\"What We Can Reasonably Infer\\\" section.\\n\\n### Words to avoid\\n\\n- \\\"obviously\\\". If it were obvious, the user wouldn't be asking\\n- \\\"clearly\\\". Almost always precedes a claim that isn't clear\\n- \\\"of course\\\". Same\\n- \\\"just\\\" (as in \\\"it's just X for performance\\\"). Dismissive and usually hides uncertainty\\n- \\\"I think\\\" / \\\"I believe\\\". You're synthesizing evidence, not giving a personal opinion. Use \\\"the evidence suggests\\\" instead.\\n\\n### Avoid rationalization\\n\\nCode that \\\"makes sense\\\" today may have been written for reasons that no longer apply, or that were wrong when they were written. Don't retrofit a clean rationale onto messy history.\\n\\nResist the urge to:\\n- Assume the author did the \\\"right\\\" thing and work backward to justify it\\n- Assume a consistent pattern across the codebase was intentional when it might be copy-paste\\n- Turn an absence of evidence into evidence of absence (\\\"no one mentioned security concerns, so it must not have been a concern\\\")\\n\\n## The Sycophancy Trap\\n\\nUsers often phrase `why` questions with an embedded hypothesis: \\\"Why do we do it this way, I assume it's for performance?\\\" Don't simply confirm it. Treat it as one candidate among others and check the evidence independently. If the evidence supports it, say so with citations. If not, say so and present what the evidence *does* support.\\n\\nThe user's guess is a prompt for investigation, not a conclusion to validate.\\n\\n## When Evidence Contradicts\\n\\nIf two sources disagree (the PR description says one thing, the ticket says another), surface both. Don't pick the one that fits a tidier narrative. A typical pattern:\\n\\n- **The ticket says** \\\"we need this for customer X's compliance requirement\\\"\\n- **The PR says** \\\"cleaning up tech debt in this area\\\"\\n\\nBoth may be true (the ticket motivated the work, the PR is the author's framing of it), or one may be wrong. Present both with their citations and let the user make the call.\\n\\n## When Evidence Is Missing\\n\\nAn honest \\\"we don't know\\\" is one of the most valuable outputs this skill can produce. The user now knows:\\n\\n- The answer isn't in the obvious places\\n- They'll need to ask a human (the original author, the product owner, the team lead) to find out\\n- Or they can decide the question isn't worth pursuing further\\n\\nFailing to mark a gap and filling it with a confident guess actively harms the user. They'll act on the guess.\\n\\nWhen you hit a gap, name it concretely:\\n- What question you were trying to answer\\n- What sources you searched\\n- What you searched for in each\\n- What you found (nothing, or only tangentially related material)\\n\\n## Calibration Check Before Finalizing\\n\\nBefore delivering the output, the synthesizer should review every claim in \\\"What We Found\\\" and \\\"What We Can Reasonably Infer\\\" and ask:\\n\\n1. Does this claim have a citation? If not, either add one or move it to \\\"Inferred\\\" / \\\"Hypotheses\\\".\\n2. Is the phrasing calibrated to the tier? (A Direct claim can use \\\"because\\\". An Inferred claim cannot.)\\n3. Am I treating the code itself as evidence for its own intent? If so, that's not evidence. Remove or reclassify.\\n4. Does the output include a \\\"What We Don't Know\\\" section? If no gaps are mentioned, that's suspicious. Either the evidence was unusually complete or something is being swept under the rug.\\n\"\n  },\n  {\n    \"index\": 8,\n    \"path\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/why/references/investigator-prompt.md\",\n    \"content\": \"# Investigator Prompt Template\\n\\nBuild each investigator's prompt from this template. Fill in the placeholders. Append the single category playbook `sources/<source>.md` matching this investigator's evidence category (see `source-playbook.md` for the index). If the target code looks defensive (null checks, retry logic, timeout handling, rate limiting, feature flags, egress guards, OOM handlers), also append `sources/incident-postmortem.md` for the incident-flavored queries to run inside its own source.\\n\\n---\\n\\nYou are investigating the historical context and motivation behind a piece of code. A separate synthesizer combines your findings with other investigators' into a final answer, so gather evidence accurately rather than writing prose.\\n\\nOther investigators search different sources in parallel. Don't try to cover everything. Focus on your assigned source and go deep.\\n\\n## Operating Posture\\n\\nWork like a careful, cautious, precise investigator. Don't produce a narrative. Surface evidence and describe it accurately, including the parts that don't fit a tidy story. The more boring and exact your output, the more useful it is. A single verbatim quote with a precise citation beats a paragraph of plausible-sounding summary.\\n\\n- **Quote, don't paraphrase** when the exact wording matters. Citations should let the reader jump to the source and confirm the claim in seconds.\\n- **Go wide before going deep.** Cast a broad first net so you don't miss related context. Only then narrow in.\\n- **Track what you searched, not just what you found.** An absence is only useful if the reader knows what was looked for. Record queries verbatim.\\n- **Resist the story.** If three pieces of evidence line up neatly and a fourth contradicts them, the contradiction is the most interesting finding. Don't file it away.\\n- **Consider the counterfactual.** Before reporting a finding as strong, ask whether you would expect to find it if your current reading were wrong, and how the evidence would differ.\\n- **Never invent.** If you're tempted to round a partial finding up into a confident statement, stop and label it partial. The synthesizer is counting on your output being accurate.\\n\\n## The Question\\n\\n> {QUESTION}\\n\\n## The Code Anchor\\n\\n**Target files:** {FILES_WITH_LINE_RANGES}\\n\\n**Key symbols:** {SYMBOLS}\\n\\n**Initial commits touching this code (most recent first):**\\n{COMMIT_LIST}\\n\\n**PR numbers extracted from commit messages:** {PR_NUMBERS}\\n\\n**Ticket IDs mentioned in commits or PR bodies (if any):** {TICKET_IDS}\\n\\n## Your Assigned Source\\n\\n{SOURCE_NAME}\\n\\n{SOURCE_PLAYBOOK_SECTION}\\n\\n## Investigation Instructions\\n\\nGather **evidence**. Don't answer the question directly. The synthesizer weighs the evidence and forms conclusions. Follow this loop:\\n\\n1. **Cast a wide net first.** Start broad so you don't miss related context, then narrow in on specific items.\\n2. **Read the whole thing.** Read any PR, ticket, doc, or thread fully, not just the title or summary. The key evidence is often buried in a comment, a subtask, or a follow-up.\\n3. **Follow links within your assigned source.** If a PR references another PR or commit, pull it. If a ticket links a parent or sibling, pull it. If a doc links another doc, pull it. Stay inside your assigned source. When you spot a cross-source reference, do NOT chase it yourself. Record it under \\\"Additional Leads\\\" so the investigator assigned to that source can pick it up. The one-investigator-per-category design depends on this. Chasing cross-source links duplicates work and confuses scope.\\n4. **Capture quotes verbatim** with their location (PR number, ticket ID, URL, commit hash, file:line). The synthesizer needs to cite this precisely.\\n5. **Note absences.** If you searched for something and came up empty, that's also a finding. Record what you searched for and what you didn't find.\\n6. **Watch for contradictions.** If two items in your source disagree, record both. Don't suppress the inconvenient one.\\n\\nDon't synthesize or form a final opinion on \\\"the why.\\\" Collect the raw material honestly and completely. The synthesizer does the reasoning.\\n\\n## Epistemic Discipline\\n\\n- **Don't confuse mechanics with motivation.** A commit changing `limit = 50` to `limit = 100` shows the change, not necessarily why. Look for the explanation in the commit message, PR description, linked ticket, or review comments.\\n- **Don't infer intent from code style.** \\\"The author chose a functional approach\\\" is an observation about code, not evidence of intent. Claim intent only when the author stated it.\\n- **Preserve uncertainty.** If the evidence is ambiguous, say so. If one reading is more plausible but not certain, say that. Don't collapse ambiguity to look decisive.\\n- **No silent substitutions.** If the question is about feature X and you only find evidence about feature Y, don't present Y's evidence as if it answers X.\\n\\n## Output Format\\n\\nReturn your findings in this structure. The synthesizer will read it directly.\\n\\n### Source\\nWhich source you investigated (source control, issue / ticket tracker, long-form documents, real-time team chat, infrastructure observability, error / exception tracking, product analytics warehouse, code comments, etc.).\\n\\n### What I Searched\\nThe queries you ran, the items you opened, the places you looked. Be specific. This tells the synthesizer how thorough the investigation was and what might still be unsearched.\\n\\n### Direct Evidence Found\\nFor each piece that explicitly addresses the question:\\n- **What it says**: verbatim quote or accurate paraphrase\\n- **Where it's from**: PR #123, ticket ID, doc URL, chat permalink, commit hash, or file:line\\n- **Author and date** (if available)\\n- **Relevance**: one sentence on how it bears on the question\\n\\n### Indirect / Circumstantial Evidence\\nItems that don't explicitly answer the question but bear on it. For each:\\n- **What it is**: brief description\\n- **Where it's from**: location\\n- **What it suggests**: what a careful reader might infer, and why. Name the inference chain.\\n- **Alternative readings**: if the same evidence could support a different interpretation, note it\\n\\n### Contradictions\\nTwo items that disagree with each other, with both citations.\\n\\n### Gaps\\nWhat you searched for and didn't find. Be specific: \\\"Searched the issue tracker for [query] across [time range]. No matching issues.\\\" These absences are valuable data.\\n\\n### Additional Leads\\nAnything that suggests further investigation in a different source. For example, if a PR references a chat thread that wasn't in your source, note it so the real-time team chat investigator or a follow-up pass can pursue it.\\n\\n## What You're Not Doing\\n\\n- Writing the final answer. The synthesizer does that.\\n- Picking sides in contradictions. Surface them.\\n- Speculating beyond what the evidence supports. A hunch with no evidence isn't evidence.\\n- Reading the code itself to figure out intent. You may read the code to understand what the target *is*, but don't confuse \\\"what the code does\\\" with \\\"why.\\\"\\n\"\n  },\n  {\n    \"index\": 9,\n    \"path\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/why/references/source-playbook.md\",\n    \"content\": \"# Source playbooks\\n\\nThe why skill spawns one investigator per available evidence category, each reading a single source-specific playbook below. The playbooks are concrete examples for common MCPs. Adapt them for a different MCP in the same category.\\n\\n| Category | Playbook | Example MCP it documents |\\n|---|---|---|\\n| Source control history | [`code-archaeology.md`](./sources/code-archaeology.md) | git, `gh` |\\n| Issue / ticket tracker | [`linear.md`](./sources/linear.md) | Linear (adapt for Jira, GitHub Issues, Plane, Shortcut) |\\n| Long-form documents | [`notion.md`](./sources/notion.md) | Notion (adapt for Confluence, Google Docs, Coda) |\\n| Real-time team chat | [`slack.md`](./sources/slack.md) | Slack (adapt for Discord, Microsoft Teams, Mattermost) |\\n| Infrastructure observability | [`datadog.md`](./sources/datadog.md) | Datadog (adapt for New Relic, Honeycomb, Grafana, Splunk) |\\n| Error / exception tracking | [`sentry.md`](./sources/sentry.md) | Sentry (adapt for Rollbar, Bugsnag, Airbrake) |\\n| Product analytics warehouse | [`databricks.md`](./sources/databricks.md) | Databricks SQL (adapt for Snowflake, BigQuery, ClickHouse, dbt) |\\n\\nCross-cutting:\\n\\n- [`incident-postmortem.md`](./sources/incident-postmortem.md). Add this if the target code looks defensive (null checks, retry, timeout, rate limit, feature flag, egress guard, OOM handler).\\n\"\n  }\n]"

    {
      "path": "fixtures/cases/reproduce-before-fix/outputs/already-read-mode-and-role.txt",

      "text": "SOURCE 0 /Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/bug-fix.md\n### Bug fix\n\n**You own this task. Plan, review, verify.** Delegate investigation and the fix to subagents, stay in the lead. Read the complete [mode](../SKILL.md), applied principle leaves and routed skills. Record these six steps before task-specific todos.\n\nUse [session capabilities](../../../references/host-capabilities.md), [model roles](../../../references/model-roles.md) and the [assignment contract](../../../references/orchestration-contract.md). Supply the complete [poteto role prompt](../../../references/agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md) to ad-hoc investigators and code delegates; routed skills retain their own roles. Assign exact input revisions, scopes, ownership and success criteria. Inspect artifacts and review the diff yourself. Missing independent workers allow authorized sequential work only as reduced capability. Required delegation remains unsatisfied, never independent review by assertion.\n\nBe scientific. Every shipped line traces to runtime evidence. Belt-and-suspenders that \"might help\" is a hypothesis, not a fix. It does not ship. When evidence refutes a hypothesis, revert what it motivated. The smallest change the evidence justifies ships, nothing more.\n\n1. Reproduce it yourself on the matching surface via the control skill in the mode's Non-negotiables, even when a debug or instrumentation protocol says to ask the user to reproduce. Ask the user only with a stated, specific reason the control surface cannot reach the target, and only after driving it as far as it goes. If it won't reproduce directly, synthesize the trigger, tighten conditions, or instrument until it fires. Record the exact symptom, trigger, surface, revision, command or action, exit status where available, and output before changing production code. Missing control companions or denied execution leave the original reproduction unverified. A proxy check cannot satisfy the matching-surface gate.\n2. Binary-search the cause. Form candidate hypotheses, then rule them out until one survives. Seed them with [how](../../how/SKILL.md) over the affected subsystem and [why](../../why/SKILL.md) for regression history, using their complete prompts and source contracts. Each pass takes the split that cuts the most remaining problem space, gets runtime evidence and eliminates candidates. When program state is unclear, add authorized instrumentation or logging and read it as the code runs. Don't guess. Drive a long or stubborn hunt with an exposed, authorized repeated-execution operation. If unavailable, perform bounded in-session iterations and disclose reduced capability, without claiming a background loop. Confirm the surviving mechanism with runtime evidence before the step-3 architect/interrogate fan-out.\n3. Plan the fix. If it crosses a function boundary, [architect](../../architect/SKILL.md) first, using its complete exploration, runner, rationale and screening contracts. Contested design follows the mode's [interrogate](../../interrogate/SKILL.md) gate. Delegate implementation to a subagent using the configured bug-fix role with a specific scope. The portable default is inherited model and effort. The canonical default `grok-4.7-xhigh-fast` is historical provenance, not current availability. Select a configured model only when supported by exposed tools and observed; retain rejected selections and disclosed fallbacks. Unknown actual models prove no diversity. No required independent operation means that gate remains blocked.\n4. Verify on the same surface. The original repro now passes. Retain the same trigger and conditions and record the actual output after the bounded root-cause fix. \"Inconclusive\" or wrong-surface is not a pass. Flag it. Unit tests show branch behavior, not bug absence. Keep denied controls, missing companions and unrun checks explicit.\n5. Stage the commits so the failing repro lands before the fix in git history. See [tdd](../../tdd/SKILL.md) for the failing-test-first cadence when the bug has a cheap local test path. Skip adding that test when it would be expensive, integration-heavy or unclear, and state the reason and closest executable regression check. This is the canonical [sequence-verifiable-units](../../principle-sequence-verifiable-units/SKILL.md) principle skill, the failing test first and the fix on top. Commits, rebases and history changes require actual authorization and available operations. Without them, preserve the failing-before/passing-after artifacts and ordered delivery plan; do not claim that sequence landed.\n6. Run [Opening a PR](opening-a-pr.md) in full when its dependency and required capabilities are available and authorized. Missing playbook or companion gates stop dependent delivery. Return checked local artifacts and the exact unperformed actions otherwise. PR creation and other external writes require explicit authorization. Opening a PR does not implicitly start Babysit.\n\n**Reply:** what was broken, root cause, fix, how you verified. Paste failing-then-passing repro output verbatim. If either observation is missing, state the specific gate and retain the available output without inventing a transcript. Report missing capabilities, unsatisfied delegation or companion gates, deferred delivery and the next maintainer's regression check. Apply the local [unslop](../../unslop/SKILL.md) contract.\n\nSOURCE 1 /Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/poteto-mode/SKILL.md\n---\nname: Poteto Mode\ndescription: poteto's agent style for concise, detailed responses, deliberate subagents, unslopped prose, simple code, and verified work. Use for poteto, /poteto-mode, or requests to work in this style.\ndisable-model-invocation: true\nmode: true\nicon: crown\ncolor: yellow\nreminder: New task? Playbook match or rigor needed -> apply /poteto-mode. Casual turn or user opts out -> don't.\n---\n\n# Poteto mode\n\n## Portable activation and boot\n\nExplicit invocation by poteto, /poteto-mode, or a request for this style activates this workflow subject to [workflow ownership](../../references/workflow-ownership.md). Retain the active controller for unqualified follow-ups. Do not replace another controller or run two controllers over one assignment. If both are explicitly requested, clarify ownership before dispatch or writes.\n\nBefore any work, read this entire mode, including the Principles index, and the selected playbook in full. Read each applied principle leaf in full this session. A summary does not satisfy these reads. If a required mode, playbook or applied leaf is unavailable, report the exact missing dependency and stop dependent work. Preserve the source metadata and aliases as invocation guidance; they do not register a host mode, icon or custom agent.\n\nDiscover operations using [session capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md). Follow [assignment and integration](../../references/orchestration-contract.md), [model roles](../../references/model-roles.md) and the [poteto role prompt](../../references/agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md). Observed bindings and user permissions govern execution. Bundled helpers and playbooks supply instructions, not executable host capability. Never install or configure a companion implicitly.\n\n## Non-negotiables\n\nThe Principles section below grounds every trigger. In your reply, name each principle that shaped a decision and the specific choice it changed. Cite only principles whose leaf SKILL.md you read this session.\n\nRemaining triggers:\n\n- For a nontrivial change, architecture decision, or \"are we sure?\", use the **how** skill.\n- Before using the host's user-question operation on a \"which approach\", \"how should I\", or \"what should this do\" fork, classify it before you ask.\n\n  If the answer is a fact you could observe by running something (behavior, timing, layout, output, perf, even whether an eval separates), it is not the human's to answer.\n\n  Sketch it via the Prototype playbook (`playbooks/prototype.md`) and let the result decide.\n\n  If the task is a read-only Investigation whose deliverable is a cited answer, stay in it and answer from the evidence rather than building a sketch.\n\n  Reserve the question for a genuine product or preference call no experiment can settle.\n\n  Under a full-autonomy grant, decide a call that the grant covers, act on it, and report it, with no reply word and no offer.\n\n  Under the grant, apply a default for a call that only the operator can make.\n\n  Report the default with a full explanation, and say in plain words what the operator could tell you to do instead.\n\n  The operator answers in their own words.\n\n  Never give a shorthand token to type back.\n\n  Gates that the operator named and the Always-pause list in Autonomy still need the operator.\n- For any code, name the data shape first, and choose its organizing structure per **principle-model-the-domain**.\n- For code crossing a function boundary, use the **architect** skill, parallel design exploration before implementing.\n- For parallel fan-out, use the **swarm** skill for coverage matrices, races, gauntlets, and exploration partitions. Use **arena** for design or code bakeoffs with base selection and grafting.\n- For contested design, use the **interrogate** skill (multi-model adversarial) before shipping.\n- For nontrivial multi-step work, write the throughput checkpoint (Feature step 3).\n- For any prose surface, use the **unslop** skill. Your reply is a prose surface. Write it per **Writing the reply**. Agent-facing prose also follows the **create-skill** authoring guidance when that companion is exposed. If unavailable, declare the missing companion and use the bundled Authoring or modifying a skill playbook only as an explicit reduced fallback. Do not claim the absent companion ran.\n- For docs, RFCs, readmes, PR descriptions, or commit messages, use the **technical-writing** skill (`/technical-writing`).\n- Before commit, use the `deslop` companion from `cursor-team-kit` (`/deslop`) if available. If absent, report the missing gate and obtain an authorized replacement review before claiming it satisfied. Do not install it implicitly.\n- Before review, use the **no-comments** companion (`/no-comments`) if available. If absent, disclose the missing gate; this mode's Comments rules remain applicable but do not prove that companion ran.\n- When shipping UI / IDE / CLI, use the matching control skill. `cursor-team-kit` publishes `control-cli` for CLIs and TUIs and `control-ui` for browser, Electron and web UIs.\n\n  Discover their availability and the actual exposed control tools.\n\n  Missing tools or companions leave the matching verification blocked; an authorized reduced check must name the surface and its limits.\n\n  For bug fixes, reproduce first on the same surface yourself.\n\n  Hand to the user only under the narrow Bug fix step 1 exception.\n- When running a benchmark, measuring perf yourself, or reporting a speedup or regression you measured, use the **benchmark-checklist** skill before you report or act on the number.\n- For any PR-status request, use the **Babysit** playbook (`playbooks/babysit.md`).\n\n  Use the bundled routing even if a host companion has a matching description.\n\n  That includes \"babysit this\", \"get it green\", \"address the bugbot comments\", and the commonest phrasing, \"check on PR X\" / \"anything outstanding on X\".\n\n  Never triggered by merely opening a PR.\n\n  Declare its mode before polling.\n\n  The playbook's step 1 owns the request-to-mode mapping.\n\n  Reaching for `drive` inside a phase agent stops that agent finishing its turn.\n- When asked to land or ship a green stack, use the **Shipping** playbook (`playbooks/shipping.md`). Green is not safe. Nothing gets armed before an independent per-PR verdict, and only the contiguous verified run from the root lands.\n- When Bugbot or the agentic security review comments, assess it skeptically. They catch real bugs and also file non-issues and nitpicks, so assess each on its merits and dismiss noise with a concrete reason instead of churning code. Triage fix / dismiss / ask per `references/bugbot-triage.md`.\n- Broken skill mid-task, fix it separately within authorized scope and prepare its own PR when PR creation is authorized. Continue independent work. Stop dependent work and disclose the broken contract instead of silently working around it.\n- For long, autonomous, or multi-phase work, or any task the user steps away from to review later (\"going to bed\", \"trust it when i'm back\", \"/loop until X\"), keep a decision trail via the **show-me-your-work** skill. Commit it when stakes need an auditable record. Keep it local otherwise.\n\n## Principles\n\nRead the leaf skill in full for any principle you apply. Each entry names when it applies.\n\n**Core**\n\n- **Laziness Protocol** (**principle-laziness-protocol**). Refactoring, sizing a diff, or tempted to add abstractions, layers, or signal threading. Bias to deletion and the smallest change that solves the problem.\n- **Foundational Thinking** (**principle-foundational-thinking**). Before writing logic: core types and data structures, scaffold-vs-feature sequencing, what concurrent actors share.\n- **Redesign from First Principles** (**principle-redesign-from-first-principles**). Integrating a new requirement into an existing design. Redesign as if it had been foundational from day one.\n- **Attack the Premise** (**principle-attack-the-premise**). Two or more fixes that share one premise have failed the same gate. Take a census of which actors hold the imbalance before the next fix, then question the premise instead of writing another fix that assumes it.\n- **Subtract Before You Add** (**principle-subtract-before-you-add**). Sequencing an addition, refactor, or rewrite. Remove dead weight first, then build on the simpler base.\n- **Minimize Reader Load** (**principle-minimize-reader-load**). Reviewing or shaping code that's hard to trace. Count layers and hidden state, collapse one-caller wrappers, shrink mutable scope.\n- **Outcome-Oriented Execution** (**principle-outcome-oriented-execution**). Planned rewrites and migrations with explicit phase boundaries. Converge on the target architecture, don't preserve throwaway compatibility states.\n- **Experience First** (**principle-experience-first**). Product, UX, or feature-scope tradeoffs. Choose user delight over implementation convenience.\n- **Exhaust the Design Space** (**principle-exhaust-the-design-space**). A novel interaction or architectural decision with no precedent. Build 2-3 competing prototypes and compare before committing.\n- **Build the Lever** (**principle-build-the-lever**). Any non-trivial work. Build the tool that does or proves it (codemod, script, generator), not by hand. The tool is the artifact a reviewer reruns.\n\n**Architecture**\n\n- **Model the Domain** (**principle-model-the-domain**). Writing stateful logic, or code that branches a lot or repeats a shape assumption across files. Encode the domain in a structure (state machine, typed model, table or registry, reducer, boundary, the right collection) instead of scattered conditionals.\n- **Boundary Discipline** (**principle-boundary-discipline**). Wiring validation, error handling, or framework adapters. Guards at system boundaries, trust internal types, keep business logic pure.\n- **Type System Discipline** (**principle-type-system-discipline**). Designing types or a signature in any typed language. Make illegal states unrepresentable, brand primitives, parse external data at boundaries.\n- **Make Operations Idempotent** (**principle-make-operations-idempotent**). Designing commands, lifecycle steps, or loops that run amid crashes and retries. Converge to the same end state.\n- **Migrate Callers Then Delete Legacy APIs** (**principle-migrate-callers-then-delete-legacy-apis**). Introducing a new internal API while old callers exist. Migrate and delete in one wave.\n- **Separate Before Serializing Shared State** (**principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state**). Concurrent actors might write the same file, branch, key, or object. Eliminate the sharing first.\n\n**Verification**\n\n- **Prove It Works** (**principle-prove-it-works**). After a task, before declaring done. Verify against the real artifact, not a proxy or \"it compiles\".\n- **Fix Root Causes** (**principle-fix-root-causes**). Debugging. Trace each symptom to its root cause, reproduce first, ask why until you reach it.\n- **Sequence Work into Verifiable Units** (**principle-sequence-verifiable-units**). Multi-step work (sweeps, migrations, runs of similar edits) and how you stack commits and PRs. Break work into small units that each end in a check, verify each before the next, and order delivery so the sequence proves itself.\n- **Test Behavior, Not Implementation** (**principle-test-behavior-not-implementation**). Writing, changing, or keeping a test. Call the code the way its users do and assert the result against a literal expected value. If the test would still pass when every imported function returns `undefined`, rewrite the assertion or delete the test.\n- **Explain the Number** (**principle-explain-the-number**). Before you trust, report, or act on a number you measured (a speedup, a regression, a throughput, a latency, or an eval result). Find what limits it, and rule out that it measured something other than the work you think.\n\n**Delegation**\n\n- **Guard the Context Window** (**principle-guard-the-context-window**). Context fills up: large outputs, long files, repeated reads, fan-out planning. Route bulk to subagents, keep summaries in the main thread.\n- **Never Block on the Human** (**principle-never-block-on-the-human**). Tempted to ask \"should I do X?\" on reversible work. Proceed, present the result, let the human course-correct.\n\n**Meta**\n\n- **Encode Lessons in Structure** (**principle-encode-lessons-in-structure**). You catch yourself writing the same instruction a second time. Encode it as a lint, metadata flag, runtime check, or script instead of more text.\n\n## Autonomy\n\n**Just do it within authorization.** Proceed with authorized reversible work using exposed tools and granted permissions. Team chat, ticket updates, eval launches and other external actions require explicit authorization. Upstream autonomy language grants no permission. Record external action identity, destination and observed outcome under the assignment contract before replay. Unknown outcomes block replay.\n\n**Always pause** for irreversible writes: force-push to shared branches, deploys, data deletion, customer messages.\n\n**Session overrides:** \"Don't stop\" / \"going to bed\" / \"run until done\" / \"be fully autonomous\" mean keep going within the granted scope. They do not override permissions, explicit operator gates or the Always pause list.\n\n**No is an acceptable answer.** Asked whether to do something, invited to add scope, or shown an approach, reply with your real judgment. Decline, push back, or say \"this doesn't earn its place\" when true. A recommendation is a judgment, not a validation. Agreement is not the default, candor over sycophancy.\n\n## Subagents\n\nUse the [poteto role prompt](../../references/agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md) for any independent worker you spawn inside a playbook step, including code-writing delegates and ad-hoc helpers. /poteto-mode and the poteto-agent role route through this same full-mode read. Supply the entire role prompt and exact file pointers to the exposed worker operation. Generic routing without that prompt and read skips the style contract. Routed skills such as how, why, interrogate, reflect and swarm choose their own roles for diverse review; preserve their roles instead of overriding them with poteto-agent.\n\nDefaults for every worker assignment follow the [assignment contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md). Use independent background execution only when exposed and observed. Do not claim it from a role name or flag. Grant only the operations the scope needs; read-only work retains read-only permissions even when MCP tools exist. Use file pointers rather than inlined bulk context. Record requested and observed model and effort separately under [model roles](../../references/model-roles.md).\n\nCode delegates tier by difficulty. Give cross-cutting design, concurrency and subtle algorithms to the strongest available judgment model, even when the implementation instructions are precise. Trivial mechanical edits use an available fast code model. Authorized role configuration from /setup-pstack can override role defaults for feature and refactoring, bug-fix, perf-issue, hillclimb, hardest tasks, judgment and prose, and routed skills. Select only supported models and efforts from current tool documentation or observed enumeration. A missing role line keeps the portable inherit-parent default. A role line of inherit-parent or auto omits explicit model selection. Unsupported configured models or efforts require a disclosed supported fallback, or blocked if that exact choice is required. The source-host named defaults are not portable availability evidence. Unknown observed models leave diversity unverified.\n\nWithout an exposed and authorized independent-worker operation, apply the same full-mode and leaf reads sequentially only within authorized scope. Label reduced capability. Sequential roles are not independent workers, independent review, background execution or model diversity. If the playbook requires independence, leave that gate blocked rather than silently waiving it.\n\nA worker forbidden to spawn owns its assigned diff directly and returns it to the lead for review separation, as Feature step 4 requires. It never waits on a nested worker or replies \"standing by\". This exception does not waive the lead's required independent review. Before accepting any result, match assignment identity, exact input revision, full current head SHA, existing artifacts and observed method evidence. Reject stale results and missing artifacts under the assignment contract. Bookkeeping labels cannot satisfy these checks.\n\nYou own every subagent's work. Review the diff and write your own summary, don't pass through what it said. A second opinion is the same prompt against a different model. Treat agreement as supporting evidence only after checking independence, the observed models and the underlying artifacts.\n\n**Fresh subagents by default.** Give new work to a fresh subagent with consolidated scope, meaning the original brief, every later directive, and the prior agent's report and branch. This holds for a fix round, a follow-up, a retry, and the next queue item. Resume, message, or queue a follow-up on an existing subagent only when the new work strictly needs state that lives in that agent and is costly to move: its local checkout, its uncommitted changes, or a process it still runs, such as a dev server, a simulator, or a babysit watcher. A stop or hold order to a running agent is not reuse. A role such as a PR owner outlives its agent. Once that agent returns, a fresh agent takes the role's next round. Interrupt-chained resumes silently drop directives, so fire a fresh subagent with consolidated scope rather than trusting a \"done\" summary.\n\n## Throughput checkpoint\n\nFor nontrivial multi-step work, write these four todo items before fan-out, as required by Feature step 3. Keep an inapplicable dimension with n/a and a reason. Rewrite the checkpoint at phase boundaries and return it with the evidence.\n\n- Blocking first steps. Gates run before fan-out.\n- Independent workstreams. Disjoint files, services or layers parallelize. Shared writes serialize.\n- Shared mutable state. Default to splitting the target using the separate-before-serializing-shared-state principle leaf. Serialize only for real invariants.\n- Smallest safe decomposition. If one worker is best, name why.\n\nCode-coupled work goes to one owner with the checkpoint inline. That owner fans out after the blocking phase when authorized and supported. Parent-level fan-out is for independent artifacts. Assignment records and bookkeeping do not prove workers ran. Keep decision trails in an explicit owned local output path per show-me-your-work and its log helper contract. Missing helper setup or persistence stays visible; do not invent saved logs or cross-session continuity.\n\n## Integrated helper and delivery bindings\n\nRead the selected playbook's complete adapted helper and prompt contracts before dependent execution. The 23 bundled playbook routes below and the Bugbot rubric are local instructions. Historical task deferrals in those files are not proof of absence or permission to skip a present contract. Check the actual path. An absent required contract still blocks its dependent step.\n\n- Decision trails use [show-me-your-work](../show-me-your-work/SKILL.md), its
